The window reads what `icat patterns` reads and says it in the same words, now shared, so the two surfaces cannot drift apart in a count or a caveat. A pattern chosen there makes its processes the selection rather than marking edges, because a drawn edge stands for every port and interface between its two ends: a mark on it would claim more than the pattern holds, while a selection claims only that the processes took part, and already leads to their records with Enter. A process only a newer generation holds is left out and the window says so, rather than selecting a process it does not show.

Revision 765 adds causal navigation over the links the evidence records (`contracts/links-v1.md`, ADR-066; plan M12, §7.4, P8). A link is one of three recorded facts the capture already derives - a child's creation record naming its parent, a client call's one ALPC message reaching the server call that served it, a TCP connection's connect or accept saying which end made it - so every link can be checked against its records, and a chain of them says no more than its links. On a busy machine many processes start, call and connect in the same instant, and time alone would chain them: nearness is therefore listed beside the links, so a person sees what happened at the same moment, and never followed. A host is an address whoever was at it, so no chain runs through one, and what of a process links to nothing is counted by why rather than read as nothing it depended on.

Revision 766 follows a process's links in the window (`contracts/links-v1.md` §8; plan M12, R18). A link is followed either way along it, since a person asking what led to a process walks back along the links as often as forward, and the path walked is said above the list, so a chain read in the window is the chain the command line finds. A link to an address is never followed, because whoever was at it is not observed, and a link the evidence policy does not admit is listed but never followed, as on the command line. Nearness in time is text, not a row, so nothing in the window can be followed through it.

Revision 767 adds M12's bottleneck views as waits (`contracts/waits-v1.md`, ADR-067; plan M12, R3). A wait is a client call's validated span - its start and stop paired on the caller - because that is what the capture measured; a call missing either is counted, never estimated. Waits are grouped by caller, interface and the process that served them through ALPC, and the server's own time is set beside the caller's only over calls linked to a server call that completed, so a share compares two ends of the same call. The capture records no thread's waits and no nesting of calls, so a long call may have run beside other work: no wait is said to have blocked or delayed anything, and no path through calls is called critical.

Revision 768 adds M12's optional outlier detection (`contracts/outliers-v1.md`, ADR-068; plan M12, R18, R21). The plan asks that it explain its baseline window, minimum sample size and uncertainty. A range fitted from the windows' mean and spread was tried first and rejected: records arrive in bursts, and simulated over bursty arrivals such a 99% range put as many as 5 rows in 100 above it by chance where it claimed 1 in 200, on the counts and on their square roots or logarithms alike. So the baseline is cut into windows as long as the compared interval, and a row is an outlier only where its records are above or below every window's: were the compared interval one more window, that happens at most 1 time in one more than the windows, whatever the records' distribution. The minimum sample is 19 windows, 1 in 20 at worst, and the reading says how many rows chance alone would put outside their range. Each label says what was counted - above, below or within every window - and never why.

Revision 769 lists the waits in the window (`contracts/waits-v1.md` §7; plan M12, R18). It reads the same reading as `icat waits` and says it in the same words, so the window adds no measure of its own. A wait is a caller's calls served by one process, so choosing it selects both processes together, as a pattern's processes are selected (ADR-065), and never the edge between them, which stands for every call and connection between the two; a wait no server is named for goes to its caller alone. Opened with a process selected, the list starts with that process's calls and the calls it served, since the selection is usually the question being asked.

Revision 770 judges outliers in the window (`contracts/outliers-v1.md` §7; plan M12, R14, R15, R18). The window's time scope is the compared interval, and its baseline all the session keeps before it, as the command line's default, or all after it, so a scope near the session's start can be judged too. A rank among windows is easiest to believe when the windows are seen, so the chosen row's are drawn bar by bar beside the scope's, with lines at their most and fewest; the bars are on one scale and said in words one at a time, so nothing rests on colour or on sight alone. The row chosen is shown as a comparison's row is shown, since an outlier is a comparison's row judged otherwise.
